Run a standard all-codex implementation plan for VibeComfy/Wan2GP feature parity across the coordinated workspace repos: vibecomfy, reigh-worker, reigh-worker-orchestrator, and runpod-lifecycle.

Goal: reach feature-level parity between the Wan2GP path and VibeComfy path for all app-exposed image/video/generation capabilities. VibeComfy workflows must be pure Python ready templates, not JSON execution. Every supported workflow should have a contract, route selection, static validation, model/custom-node asset validation, live evidence, and docs. Unsupported routes must fail closed with explicit blockers, not silently drift.

User bar: no hidden or suspended functionality if it works in Wan2GP; VibeComfy should ultimately implement every applicable capability. - VibeComfy branch: megaplan/production-parity-templates. Durable branch already has tooling improvements: ready-template index, workflow parity docs, artifact metadata, embedded output resolution, SAM2 prefetch, native WanAnimate frame binding. Local VibeComfy validation slice previously passed: tests/test_ready_templates.py tests/test_workflow_core.py tests/test_model_assets.py tests/test_porting_assets.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_porting_workbench.py -> 115 passed, 1 skipped.
- Worker branch: megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix. Currently has uncommitted edits in docs/vibecomfy-app-parity-tracker.md, scripts/capability_contracts/loaders.py, and tests/test_capability_contracts.py that clear stale template-index blockers and add a regression check. Worker test tests/test_capability_contracts.py previously passed: 12 passed.
- Orchestrator branch: megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-07-orchestrator-pools-artifacts.

Root blocker identified by main thread: durable worker still marks LTX first/last travel routes fail-closed in source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py, while VibeComfy has Python LTX first/last templates in ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py and related LTX templates. - Avoid launching expensive RunPod jobs unless execution explicitly requires it and there is high confidence it will not duplicate main-thread work. If launched, terminate pods and record evidence.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: VibeComfy/Wan2GP Parity Closure With Complete LTX Rollout

## Overview
The critique does not point to a different root cause; it confirms the right root cause is still route/contract/template parity drift for LTX first/last travel. The gap is that the prior plan did not name every repository location that already treats the non-distilled `ltx2` route as VibeComfy-supported, and it targeted a generated VibeComfy ready-template file without specifying a durable edit path.

Revised approach: make the non-distilled `ltx2` rollback explicit across all current support surfaces, make the distilled template promotable only through durable VibeComfy changes, and put scratchpad-binding tests in the file that already covers the adapter writer. The non-distilled route remains fail-closed unless a true non-distilled template or tested model-switching path is implemented. The distilled route may be promoted only after doctor, model assets, adapter binding, contracts, matrix metadata, tracker docs, and static tests agree.

The plan remains scoped to the original goal: VibeComfy/Wan2GP feature parity with pure Python ready templates, fail-closed unsupported routes, explicit contracts, static validation, model/custom-node validation, live evidence discipline, and docs. No RunPod live jobs are required for this static correction slice.

## Phase 1: Make Non-Distilled LTX Fail Closed Everywhere

### Step 1: Audit and update all non-distilled support locations (`reigh-worker`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` so `travel_segment__model-ltx2__guidance-none__continuity-first_last__profile-default` is `VIBECOMFY_UNSUPPORTED` unless a true non-distilled implementation is added in the same slice.
2. Update `scripts/capability_contracts/contracts.json` so the same non-distilled route is `implementation: unsupported`, `status: unsupported_fail_closed`, with a blocker that the available LTX first/last ready template uses distilled assets and does not preserve `ltx2_22B` semantics.
3. Update `scripts/live_test/matrix.py` so `travel_segment_ltx2_first_last` is no longer treated as a VibeComfy-backed supported case. Either convert it to an explicit fail-closed/discoverability case or remove it from supported VibeComfy live-run selection.
4. Update `scripts/capability_contracts/live_matrix_manifest.json` to match the current matrix stable fields after the non-distilled case change.
5. Update `docs/vibecomfy-app-parity-tracker.md` by rendering from contracts after the contract edits.
6. Update `tests/test_template_routing.py`, especially `test_section3a_ltx_first_last_rows_route_to_python_anchor_template`, so it no longer asserts that `ltx2_22B` routes to the distilled first/last template.

### Step 2: Keep distilled and control-route expectations separate (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Keep `travel_segment__model-ltx2_distilled__guidance-none__continuity-first_last__profile-default` as the only LTX first/last route eligible for VibeComfy promotion in this slice.
2. Keep all LTX control-guide route keys fail-closed until there is control-capable VibeComfy template evidence.
3. Add or adjust tests so non-distilled, distilled, and control-guide outcomes are covered by separate assertions.

### Step 3: Fix contract and manifest drift gates (`reigh-worker/scripts/capability_contracts/`, `reigh-worker/tests/test_capability_contracts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Ensure `python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report validate --json` reports no route-binding drift after the fail-closed and manifest updates.
2. Ensure `tests/test_capability_contracts.py` passes with current `build_matrix()` stable fields.
3. Keep the regression coverage that detects future worker route, matrix, manifest, and contract drift.

## Phase 2: Make VibeComfy Template Edits Durable

### Step 4: Choose the durable edit path for the generated LTX ready template (`vibecomfy/ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py`, generator/source artifacts)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Before editing `ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py`, account for its `# vibecomfy: generated` header.
2. Prefer updating the generator inputs or override metadata used by `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` so model assets and doctor-safe structure survive regeneration.
3. If the required fix is intentionally manual and cannot be represented in generator inputs, change the first-line marker to `# vibecomfy: manual` before direct edits, as the file header instructs.
4. Do not make direct generated-file edits that would be silently overwritten on regeneration.

### Step 5: Make doctor a hard static gate (`vibecomfy/ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py` if needed)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run `python -m vibecomfy.cli doctor video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame --json` and inspect the GetNode/SetNode broadcast runtime-link errors.
2. Fix the template or doctor only where the graph is genuinely invalid or doctor is incorrectly treating valid ready-template policy rewrites as invalid.
3. Keep `python -m vibecomfy.cli validate video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame` passing.
4. Do not promote any route using this template while doctor exits nonzero.

### Step 6: Declare LTX model assets explicitly (`vibecomfy`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add durable model asset declarations for every concrete LTX model file referenced by the template: UNET/GGUF, CLIP/projection, video/audio/tiny VAE, spatial upscaler, and any distilled LoRA actually used.
2. Keep `READY_REQUIREMENTS["custom_nodes"]` aligned with the template node classes.
3. Add a focused model-asset test that fails if this ready template references model filenames absent from `READY_METADATA["model_assets"]` or `READY_REQUIREMENTS["models"]`.
4. Run `tests/test_ready_templates.py` and `tests/test_model_assets.py` before worker contract promotion.

## Phase 3: Prove Worker Binding Through the Correct Tests

### Step 7: Test the actual scratchpad writer (`reigh-worker/source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py`, `reigh-worker/tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add scratchpad-writer tests in `tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`, not only in backend-selection tests.
2. Prove the distilled LTX path preserves first image, last image, prompt, negative prompt, seed, resolution, frame count, and FPS through the generated/patched scratchpad.
3. Assert that the non-distilled model does not silently use the distilled template path.

### Step 8: Keep backend-selection tests focused (`reigh-worker/tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Use backend-selection tests only to prove route/backend dispatch behavior.
2. Do not rely on backend-selection tests as the primary proof of scratchpad field binding.

## Phase 4: Promote Only the Distilled Route

### Step 9: Promote the distilled no-control route contract after static gates are green (`reigh-worker/scripts/capability_contracts/contracts.json`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Promote only `travel_segment__model-ltx2_distilled__guidance-none__continuity-first_last__profile-default` to `implementation: vibecomfy` once doctor, model-asset, template-index, and adapter-binding tests pass.
2. Set `template_id` to `video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame`.
3. Use `status: static_valid` or `template_bound`, not `runtime_green`, until completed live evidence exists.
4. Update artifact contract fields for child task completion, MP4 output, storage path, and output artifact expectations.

### Step 10: Render tracker and verify docs (`reigh-worker/docs/vibecomfy-app-parity-tracker.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Run `python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report render docs/vibecomfy-app-parity-tracker.md` after contract edits.
2. Confirm the tracker distinguishes: distilled no-control static/template-bound, non-distilled fail-closed, and LTX control routes fail-closed.
3. Confirm no stale blocker claims remain for templates that are present and validated.

## Phase 5: Optional Runtime Evidence

### Step 11: Keep live matrix evidence disciplined (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/`, `reigh-worker/scripts/capability_contracts/live_matrix_manifest.json`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Use current matrix case names only; do not resurrect `travel_segment_ltx2_control_video_first_last`.
2. Keep live evidence empty until an actual completed report with output artifact exists.
3. Do not mark the distilled route `runtime_green` from static validation alone.

### Step 12: Launch live runs only when non-duplicative (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Launch targeted RunPod/live matrix work only if all static gates are green and the run will not duplicate main-thread live-test work.
2. If launched, record pod IDs, report paths, final statuses, output artifact locations, and termination evidence.
3. Promote to `runtime_green` only for routes with completed reports and output artifacts.

## Execution Order
1. Update every non-distilled support location in `reigh-worker`: route source, contracts, matrix, manifest, tracker, and routing tests.
2. Stabilize contract and matrix validation before relying on the test suite.
3. Make VibeComfy template edits durable through generator inputs/overrides or a manual marker.
4. Fix doctor and model-asset declarations for the distilled LTX first/last template.
5. Prove scratchpad binding in `tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`.
6. Promote only the distilled no-control route contract.
7. Render tracker docs and run static/test gates.
8. Add runtime evidence only after static gates pass and live execution is intentionally scheduled.

## Validation Order
1. `cd reigh-worker && python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report validate --json`
2. `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_capability_contracts.py -q`
3. `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py -q`
4. `cd vibecomfy && python -m tools.refresh_template_index --check`
5. `cd vibecomfy && python -m vibecomfy.cli validate video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame`
6. `cd vibecomfy && python -m vibecomfy.cli doctor video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame --json`
7. `cd vibecomfy && python -m pytest tests/test_ready_templates.py tests/test_model_assets.py -q`
8. `cd reigh-worker-orchestrator && python -m pytest tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_route_aware_task_counts.py tests/test_route_stale_worker_state.py -q` only if route metadata shape changes.
9. Optional runtime: targeted current distilled LTX live matrix case after all static gates are green.

## Remaining Blockers
- The non-distilled `ltx2` route must be corrected everywhere it is currently encoded as VibeComfy-supported.
- The LTX first/last template is generated; asset/doctor fixes must be made durable through generator inputs/overrides or a manual marker.
- `vibecomfy doctor` must pass before any route using the template is promoted.
- The template needs explicit model-asset declarations for concrete LTX model files.
- Scratchpad field binding must be proven in `tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`.
- LTX control routes remain unsupported until a control-capable VibeComfy path is proven.
- Runtime evidence is still required before any LTX route can be marked `runtime_green`.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",

    },

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: route support states: the revised step 5 says to classify ltx control rows as vibecomfy_supported, wgp_only, vibecomfy_unsupported, new, or blocked, but the current worker routesupportstate enum at reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:17-20 only supports wgp_only, vibecomfy_supported, and vibecomfy_unsupported. the plan needs to either extend the enum/report schema for new/blocked or keep new/blocked strictly as fixture disposition values mapped to an existing runtime support state.",

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-155",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

        Escalated debt subsystems:
        [
  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 9,
    "plan_count": 6,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-156",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-164",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: route support states: the revised step 5 says to classify ltx control rows as vibecomfy_supported, wgp_only, vibecomfy_unsupported, new, or blocked, but the current worker routesupportstate enum at reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:17-20 only supports wgp_only, vibecomfy_supported, and vibecomfy_unsupported. the plan needs to either extend the enum/report schema for new/blocked or keep new/blocked strictly as fixture disposition values mapped to an existing runtime support state.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-9-control-rail-and-20260506-1802"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-165",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: startup tests: step 11 still needs sharper wording for `vibecomfy_memory_profile`. the current startup template exports it only inside `if [ \"$reigh_backend\" = \"vibecomfy\" ] && [[ \"$reigh_worker_profile\" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]` at worker_startup.template.sh lines 28-30, while the revised plan says to assert it is exported consistently when proving both wgp and vibecomfy render from the same template. if implemented as an unconditional wgp+vibecomfy export assertion, the test would conflict with the current template's intentional conditional behavior.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-12-dual-executor-20260507-0226"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 6,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-142",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-154",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-158",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

        ]

      {
        "id": "DEBT-162",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",

  {

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",

        "id": "DEBT-109",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

Every supported workflow should have a contract, route selection, static validation, model/custom-node asset validation, live evidence, and docs.         Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: VibeComfy/Wan2GP Parity Closure With Atomic LTX Promotion

## Overview
The remaining critique does not identify a wrong root cause or wrong code area. The root cause is still LTX route, contract, template, and evidence drift across VibeComfy and `reigh-worker`. The remaining issue is ordering: worker contract validation cannot be green while a route is `vibecomfy_supported` but its capability contract is still `unsupported_fail_closed`.

Revised approach: use two explicit states. First, make both LTX first/last routes fail-closed while VibeComfy doctor, model assets, and adapter-binding proof are fixed. Then promote the distilled route by flipping worker route source and `contracts.json` together in one validation slice. The non-distilled route remains fail-closed unless a real non-distilled template or tested model-switching path is added. Runtime evidence remains separate; static promotion must not imply `runtime_green`.

The plan remains scoped to the original goal: VibeComfy/Wan2GP feature parity with pure Python ready templates, fail-closed unsupported routes, explicit contracts, route selection, static validation, model/custom-node validation, live evidence discipline, and docs. No RunPod live jobs are required for this static correction slice.

## Phase 1: Establish A Green Fail-Closed Baseline

### Step 1: Make current LTX first/last unsupported state consistent (`reigh-worker`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` so both `travel_segment__model-ltx2__guidance-none__continuity-first_last__profile-default` and `travel_segment__model-ltx2_distilled__guidance-none__continuity-first_last__profile-default` are `VIBECOMFY_UNSUPPORTED` during the static-fix phase.
2. In `scripts/capability_contracts/contracts.json`, keep or set both routes to `implementation: unsupported`, `status: unsupported_fail_closed`.
3. For the non-distilled route, record the blocker that the available first/last template uses distilled assets and does not preserve `ltx2_22B` semantics.
4. For the distilled route, record the blocker that promotion is pending VibeComfy doctor, model-asset declarations, and worker adapter binding proof.
5. Keep all LTX control-guide route keys fail-closed until separate control-capable VibeComfy evidence exists.

### Step 2: Update every current support surface to match fail-closed state (`reigh-worker`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `scripts/live_test/matrix.py` so `travel_segment_ltx2_first_last` and `travel_segment_ltx2_distilled_first_last` are not advertised as supported VibeComfy live-run cases during the static-fix phase. Keep them as explicit fail-closed/discoverability cases only if the matrix supports unsupported cases cleanly.
2. Update `scripts/capability_contracts/live_matrix_manifest.json` to match `build_matrix()` stable fields after the case-state changes.
3. Update `tests/test_template_routing.py`, especially `test_section3a_ltx_first_last_rows_route_to_python_anchor_template`, so it asserts fail-closed behavior for non-promoted LTX first/last routes.
4. Render `docs/vibecomfy-app-parity-tracker.md` from contracts after edits.

### Step 3: Prove the baseline validator is green (`reigh-worker/scripts/capability_contracts/`, `reigh-worker/tests/test_capability_contracts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Run `python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report validate --json` and require zero route-binding or matrix-manifest errors.
2. Run `python -m pytest tests/test_capability_contracts.py -q` and require it to pass.
3. Keep the regression coverage that detects future worker route, matrix, manifest, and contract drift.

## Phase 2: Make The Distilled VibeComfy Template Promotable

### Step 4: Choose the durable edit path for the generated ready template (`vibecomfy/ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py`, generator/source artifacts)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Before editing `ready_templates/video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame.py`, account for its `# vibecomfy: generated` header.
2. Prefer updating generator inputs or override metadata used by `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` so model assets and doctor-safe structure survive regeneration.
3. If the required fix is intentionally manual and cannot be represented in generator inputs, change the first-line marker to `# vibecomfy: manual` before direct edits.
4. Do not make generated-file edits that would be silently overwritten on regeneration.

### Step 5: Make doctor a hard static gate (`vibecomfy`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run `python -m vibecomfy.cli doctor video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame --json` and inspect the GetNode/SetNode broadcast runtime-link errors.
2. Fix the template or doctor only where the graph is genuinely invalid or doctor is incorrectly treating valid ready-template policy rewrites as invalid.
3. Keep `python -m vibecomfy.cli validate video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame` passing.
4. Do not promote any route using this template while doctor exits nonzero.

### Step 6: Declare LTX model assets explicitly (`vibecomfy`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add durable model asset declarations for every concrete LTX model file referenced by the template: UNET/GGUF, CLIP/projection, video/audio/tiny VAE, spatial upscaler, and any distilled LoRA actually used.
2. Keep `READY_REQUIREMENTS["custom_nodes"]` aligned with the template node classes.
3. Add a focused model-asset test that fails if this ready template references model filenames absent from `READY_METADATA["model_assets"]` or `READY_REQUIREMENTS["models"]`.
4. Run `tests/test_ready_templates.py` and `tests/test_model_assets.py` before worker route promotion.

## Phase 3: Prove Worker Binding Through The Correct Tests

### Step 7: Test the actual scratchpad writer (`reigh-worker/source/models/comfy/vibecomfy_adapter.py`, `reigh-worker/tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add scratchpad-writer tests in `tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`, not only in backend-selection tests.
2. Prove the distilled LTX path preserves first image, last image, prompt, negative prompt, seed, resolution, frame count, and FPS through the generated/patched scratchpad.
3. Assert that the non-distilled model does not silently use the distilled template path.

### Step 8: Keep backend-selection tests focused (`reigh-worker/tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Use backend-selection tests only to prove route/backend dispatch behavior.
2. Do not rely on backend-selection tests as the primary proof of scratchpad field binding.

## Phase 4: Atomically Promote The Distilled Route

### Step 9: Flip route source and contract together (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/capability_contracts/contracts.json`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Only after VibeComfy doctor, model-asset, template-index, and adapter-binding tests pass, change `travel_segment__model-ltx2_distilled__guidance-none__continuity-first_last__profile-default` to `VIBECOMFY_SUPPORTED`.
2. In the same change set, update its contract to `implementation: vibecomfy` with `template_id: video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame`.
3. Use `status: static_valid` or `template_bound`, not `runtime_green`, until completed live evidence exists.
4. Update artifact contract fields for child task completion, MP4 output, storage path, and output artifact expectations.
5. Immediately run `python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report validate --json` after the route/contract flip to catch any `implementation_mismatch` or `template_mismatch`.

### Step 10: Update matrix, manifest, tests, and docs for the promoted distilled route (`reigh-worker`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `scripts/live_test/matrix.py` so the distilled case is discoverable as the current supported VibeComfy static/live candidate.
2. Update `scripts/capability_contracts/live_matrix_manifest.json` to match matrix stable fields, without adding live evidence unless a completed report exists.
3. Update `tests/test_template_routing.py` so distilled resolves to the VibeComfy first/last template while non-distilled remains fail-closed.
4. Render `docs/vibecomfy-app-parity-tracker.md` and confirm it distinguishes distilled static/template-bound, non-distilled fail-closed, and LTX control-route fail-closed states.

## Phase 5: Optional Runtime Evidence

### Step 11: Keep live matrix evidence disciplined (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/`, `reigh-worker/scripts/capability_contracts/live_matrix_manifest.json`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Use current matrix case names only; do not resurrect `travel_segment_ltx2_control_video_first_last`.
2. Keep live evidence empty until an actual completed report with output artifact exists.
3. Do not mark the distilled route `runtime_green` from static validation alone.

### Step 12: Launch live runs only when non-duplicative (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Launch targeted RunPod/live matrix work only if all static gates are green and the run will not duplicate main-thread live-test work.
2. If launched, record pod IDs, report paths, final statuses, output artifact locations, and termination evidence.
3. Promote to `runtime_green` only for routes with completed reports and output artifacts.

## Execution Order
1. Make both LTX first/last routes fail-closed and make worker contracts, matrix, manifest, tests, and tracker agree.
2. Prove worker contract validation is green in the fail-closed baseline.
3. Make VibeComfy template edits durable through generator inputs/overrides or a manual marker.
4. Fix doctor and model-asset declarations for the distilled first/last template.
5. Prove scratchpad binding in `tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`.
6. Flip the distilled route source and contract together in one validation slice.
7. Update matrix, manifest, tests, and tracker for the promoted distilled route.
8. Add runtime evidence only after static gates pass and live execution is intentionally scheduled.

## Validation Order
1. Fail-closed baseline: `cd reigh-worker && python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report validate --json`
2. Fail-closed baseline: `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_capability_contracts.py tests/test_template_routing.py -q`
3. VibeComfy static: `cd vibecomfy && python -m tools.refresh_template_index --check`
4. VibeComfy static: `cd vibecomfy && python -m vibecomfy.cli validate video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame`
5. VibeComfy static: `cd vibecomfy && python -m vibecomfy.cli doctor video/ltx2_3_runexx_first_last_frame --json`
6. VibeComfy static: `cd vibecomfy && python -m pytest tests/test_ready_templates.py tests/test_model_assets.py -q`
7. Worker adapter: `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py -q`
8. Atomic promotion check: `cd reigh-worker && python -m scripts.capability_contracts.report validate --json`
9. Promotion tests: `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_capability_contracts.py tests/test_template_routing.py tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py tests/test_vibecomfy_backend_selection.py -q`
10. Orchestrator smoke only if route metadata shape changes: `cd reigh-worker-orchestrator && python -m pytest tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_route_aware_task_counts.py tests/test_route_stale_worker_state.py -q`
11. Optional runtime: targeted current distilled LTX live matrix case after all static gates are green.

## Remaining Blockers
- The current worker route/contract state must first be made internally consistent, either unsupported everywhere or supported with a matching VibeComfy contract.
- The non-distilled `ltx2` route must remain fail-closed unless a true non-distilled implementation is added.
- The LTX first/last template is generated; asset/doctor fixes must be made durable through generator inputs/overrides or a manual marker.
- `vibecomfy doctor` must pass before the distilled route is promoted.
- The template needs explicit model-asset declarations for concrete LTX model files.
- Scratchpad field binding must be proven in `tests/test_vibecomfy_adapter.py`.
- LTX control routes remain unsupported until a control-capable VibeComfy path is proven.
- Runtime evidence is still required before any LTX route can be marked `runtime_green`.

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",

      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-wan-single-frame-and-20260506-0818"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],
